The founder explicitly requests ledger-first reuse of verified prior work. Research: [Google index usage and Query Explain](https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/query-explain), [Apple sheets exit/Cancel](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/sheets), [NASA intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/7150/pages/16449673/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation).**

**2026-10-16 OPERATOR INCOMING REQUEST EXPLICIT-DECLINE CORRECTION — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Audited actual Commercial `app/operator/index.tsx` request sheet and `src/state/OperatorContext.tsx`. Existing source renders Traveler name, pickup/destination, server-derived estimated Operator retained Fare, 15-second foreground response window, Accept Traveler, Decline, and normal missed-request re-offer. **Real defect identified:** the Decline button called the *timeout* `closeRequest()` path, whose implementation only calls `op.lapseRequest()` and leaves the Travel assigned; this is not an intentional rejection and can delay matching despite a deliberate user tap. Candidate preserves ordinary countdown lapse/re-offer but routes explicit Decline to already-existing authoritative `/travel/progress` refusal through `op.declineRequest()`. Do not close the visible sheet until backend confirms; errors keep the offer and display retry; guard simultaneous Accept/Decline and timeout. The server and payment rules are unchanged, no new API/reads/fees, no driver or real Traveler synthetic actions. Twice-run source regression asserts server acknowledgment, duplicate guards, and separate timeout behavior. **Additional verified UX defects:** the 15-second numeral restarted on phone resume but its animated progress bar stayed stopped; restoring both avoids contradictory visual cues. The fallback English-only `Pickup` / `Destination` labels now reuse existing five-language keys, and small low-contrast route and revenue labels use readable existing color/size tokens. No fabricated pickup ETA, hidden live fee, external request or new screen was added. Test on installed iOS and Android with delayed/failing networking and confirm live provider behavior before deployment. Research: [Apple feedback](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/feedback), [NN/g visibility of system status](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/visibility-system-status/), [Amazon idempotent retries](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/making-retries-safe-with-idempotent-APIs/), [NASA intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/SWEHBVD/pages/102695440/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation). Council is a reference rubric, not a literal consultation.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED TRAVEL MULTIPLE BOOKINGS & CANCELLATION AUTHORITY — UNMERGED PR #52:** Complete service audit found a concrete cross-layer defect: Firestore rules allowed an authenticated Traveler to directly delete their scheduled reservation, even during or after dispatch, evading the server's atomic reserved-only cancellation and destroying evidence. Draft candidate now denies all direct scheduled-rides client writes and adversarial emulator tests assert both reserved/dispatched deletions and forged cancellation are refused. The existing server's protected transaction remains the sole cancellation authority. The client also fetched all years of scheduled records and displayed only one future Travel. Candidate adds bounded per-Traveler future-index queries, 20-booking paginated Upcoming Travel with individual status and deliberate per-record cancellation, links from Home and Menu, and a saved-payment-method entry from the scheduling review; no paid provider or changed fare. Google Firestore documentation supports ordered cursor pagination, but **the new `scheduled_rides(travelerUid,atMs)` composite index has NOT been verified READY in live Firebase**. This is a new required release gate, plus exact-SHA six workflows, real device and sandbox end-to-end tests. An already confirmed booking cannot yet be edited atomically; do not claim an Edit booking feature or a refund after assignment. [Research and test audit](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). Do not merge or deploy merely because source/emulator tests pass.

**2026-10-17 COMPLETE SCHEDULED-TRAVEL SERVICE REVIEW — DRAFT PR #52:** Founder corrected a screen-centric, arbitrary-resizing review: design must start from the full customer experience, existing shared visual rubric, vehicle choice and post-booking management. Independently read current Uber Reserve, Lyft Scheduled Rides, Blacklane workflow, Apple HIG, Nielsen Norman Group consistency, GOV.UK review and Android accessibility, and cross-checked existing American Rider code. The scheduling summary now exposes actual bookable class and party/person, with Change rows linking to the existing Travel Confirmation selector and one authoritative server quote (no extra vendor calls). Restored the shared 44-unit visual sizing rather than making this screen inconsistent; safe Android touch widths still require physical device measurements. Home now requires explicit Keep/Cancel decision before server-confirmed cancellation of only a reserved/unassigned Travel, and the Scheduled travel menu stops routing to a new booking form. Cancellation after dispatch belongs to the actual active Travel lifecycle. Existing cancellation can be free while genuinely reserved; do not import competitor fees. **Outstanding product capability, not silently claimed:** editing an already accepted reservation is not implemented as a server-authoritative priced transactional operation. Do not add a decorative Edit button, unpriced client-side mutation or duplicate bookings. Tests and [whole-service audit](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md) added. Require six exact-head CI gates and signed iPhone/Android UX, cancellation-race and sandbox-payment proof before merging or publishing; this candidate does not change geography, fares, money or real-world approvals. **Additional verified architectural gap:** `src/backend/scheduled.ts` currently reads the Traveler's entire scheduled history without a limit, then displays only the earliest active reservation. This can increase Firestore reads over account lifetime and hides the remaining future reservations from management. A safe upcoming-reservations list requires an indexed, bounded, pageable query plus a status-aware management screen, new authentic index commissioning and regression; do not claim this is already implemented.

**2026-10-17 INSTITUTION-FIRST PLAN AHEAD FOLLOW-UP — UNMERGED REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Reviewed published MIT Sloan digital customer co-design, Harvard Business School service reliability, Stanford d.school/IDEO design validation, Apple picker and accessibility guidance, Nielsen Norman Group progressive disclosure, Baymard mobile checkout clarity, Google Firestore efficiency, Amazon retry identity, and NASA requirements validation against current Plan Ahead and the Council standard. Confirmed one editable itinerary, absolute pickup-zone calendar, one authoritative Total, existing quote/search reuse, no new recurring reads, and server-side scheduling identity. Found and corrected two further user-trust defects: a booked confirmation described as relative “Tomorrow” despite midnight rollover; and AM/PM selection silently replacing a previously chosen 6:00 with 6:30. The saved appointment now carries a visible exact weekday/date/year, and changing AM/PM preserves its hour. A further recovery fix preserves a previously chosen custom day and calendar month after returning from destination search. The final Schedule Travel review now also repeats Pickup location, Destination, exact scheduled day/time and the server Total together, correcting the former date-only “Pickup” summary and preventing missing context on narrow phones. Twice-run regression updated; [research and acceptance matrix](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). This records researched recommendations, not interviews or endorsements. Pending six checks on exact final SHA plus real physical iPhone/Android visual, enlarged-text, screen-reader, narrow calendar-touch and signed booking evidence. **Do not merge PR #52 or deploy based on simulated tests alone.** No pricing, paid money, geography, insurance, screening or operator qualification changed.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED PICKUP LOOKUP STATE — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Further whole-screen audit caught another misleading state: while the app was still checking whether the selected pickup area could schedule Travel, it could temporarily display an unavailable warning. The screen now shows a neutral **Checking pickup area…** message while that existing market lookup is pending, and reserves the actual unavailable wording for completed failures. Five translations and twice-run source tests added, with **no extra network requests, Firestore reads, prices or vendors**. This addresses the user journey, not merely the original screenshot. A confirmed failed pickup-area lookup now offers **Try again**, which repeats the existing public market check only on the Traveler's tap, rather than polling or adding routine reads. This avoids a dead-end after transient connection errors. Physical-device testing is still required.

**2026-10-17 FULL PLAN AHEAD ITINERARY AND HUMAN-CENTERED UX — PR #52 REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Founder requested a whole-screen redesign, not merely the false time warning. **Source-verified Home entry legitimately starts with a chosen time before destination; that choice is preserved.** Published [Apple design/layout/picker/accessibility](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/design-principles/), [NN/g user journeys](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/user-journeys-vs-user-flows/), [Baymard mobile checkout](https://baymard.com/research-articles/current-state-of-checkout-ux), [GOV.UK dates](https://design-system.service.gov.uk/patterns/dates/), [IDEO journey mapping](https://www.designkit.org/methods/journey-map.html), [Stanford d.school](https://dschool.stanford.edu/tools/design-project-guide), [HBS service excellence](https://www.hbs.edu/faculty/Pages/profile.aspx?facId=6587), Google document-read costing, Amazon idempotent retries and NASA SWE-055 formed the decision record **before** UI changes, then were cross-checked against the ledger and actual code. The Plan ahead screen now visibly includes **two distinct editable rows, Pickup and Destination, at the top in one itinerary card**. Each uses existing Travel Confirmation search, retaining the chosen time and returning without immediate paid Travel. Today/Tomorrow/Choose date remain, with explicit calendar date for midnight clarity. A compact selected-time control reveals AM/PM, quick choices and custom quarter-hour entry only on demand. Complete Travel Cost is **Not quoted yet** until the current route and class receive the authoritative server quote; one primary button reads **Choose destination**, **Review Travel Cost**, or **Schedule Travel**, by state. Friendly pickup-local time is shown instead of IANA technical naming. Five languages and two-pass synthetic regressions cover hierarchy, fields, navigation, 44-point-high touch controls, screen reader labels, server quote, route pin, DST, clock, midnight, no extra reads and no accidental immediate confirmation. Full decisions and provenance: [Scheduled Travel UX review](docs/audits/SCHEDULED-TRAVEL-UX-2026-10-17.md). **No new SDK, vendor, billing/price engine, fee, live reservation, TestFlight or production deploy.** The PR must pass exact-head CI and real iPhone/Android usability/accessibility checks before merge; source and synthetic tests are not user-device acceptance. Council is used as a published-reference rubric, not actual personal endorsements.

**2026-10-17 SCHEDULED ITINERARY INTERACTION CHECK — PR #52:** The improved one-card pickup/destination itinerary uses the existing Travel Confirmation editors and a compact optional time selector. A follow-up inspection caught an unhandled `pickup=1` parameter: tapping **Edit pickup** would previously open Travel Confirmation without activating its pickup search. The destination and pickup editing paths now both activate the correct existing fields and preserve the scheduled-Travel return; a two-pass regression asserts link, route parameter and editor activation. No new search vendor, data collection, Stripe action or booking. Release/device acceptance still pending.

**2026-10-16 SCHEDULED TRAVEL FALSE TIME/PAYMENT VALIDATION — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Founder-supplied iPhone screen shows **Tomorrow · 6:00 AM**, home pickup **18631 SW 97th Pl**, a blank destination, **$0.00** and the error **“It is 11:36 PM, so these times are no longer available. Choose PM or another day.”** Source inspection established no evidence that tomorrow's 6 AM was actually rejected as past. `app/schedule.tsx` combined genuine past-time, absent server Travel Total, missing pickup zone and incomplete date into one `schedInvalid` Boolean; rendered the same expired-time notice for all; and printed `fmt(ride.travelerTotal)` even though zero is its “no current server quote” sentinel. The Home “Schedule Travel” button intentionally permits selecting a day **before** choosing a destination (founder-settled 2026-09-13), so missing destination at that moment is an expected, recoverable state, **not an error**. Research-first correction follows [NN/g inline forms and error-message guidance](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/errors-forms-design-guidelines/), [Apple HIG restrained actionable alerts](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/alerts), [Expo 57 Router navigation](https://docs.expo.dev/versions/v57.0.0/sdk/router/), [Google Firestore billable reads](https://cloud.google.com/firestore/pricing), [Amazon idempotent retries](https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/making-retries-safe-with-idempotent-APIs/), and [NASA SWE-055 intended-environment validation](https://swehb.nasa.gov/spaces/SWEHBVD/pages/102695440/SWE-055%2B-%2BRequirements%2BValidation). The proposed client `scheduleReadiness` separates missing destination, absent **authoritative, class-matched** Total, pickup zone/date, unconfirmed custom times, DST invalidity and real expired instants. On screenshot state it shows a destination-selection action and a dash rather than a fabricated $0 quote or time error; choosing a destination uses the **existing** Travel Confirmation search/quote pathway. The destination search now carries a lightweight `scheduling=1` **screen-navigation hint** (not a booking or payment instruction), so Travel Confirmation says **Scheduled pickup / Continue** instead of the incorrect **Leaving now / Change** while returning to the already selected day and time. No additional API requests are created. **Further end-to-end review repaired two linked failures:** destination search returning to Travel Confirmation now presents a clear **Continue to Schedule** action, not the immediate-money **Confirm Travel** button (five languages); and scheduled reservations now transmit the current map-selected pickup pin instead of stale old route coordinates. Current route geometry and completed pickup repricing are required before treating a quote as ready. The submit button rechecks the actual clock at tap, and the displayed time-zone abbreviation is calculated for the **selected pickup instant** (including DST) rather than today's abbreviation. The market time-zone lookup now uses the current selected pickup pin (or the quoted route pickup), not a stale named departure; this preserves national time-zone correctness after pin movement and uses the existing cached market lookup. These changes do not revise amounts or create new network queries. Existing midnight-aware market-local date resolution and server schedule/price authority remain intact; a clock-only 30-second foreground update and phone-resume update introduce **zero new network calls or Firestore reads**. The previous blanket warning is replaced by genuine time validation across five languages; typing an invalid or merely suggested time no longer silently schedules the old selected slot. Two-pass source and pure-state tests cover screenshot at 23:36 EDT, tomorrow 06:00, boundary instants, missing inputs and DST overlap/gap. ****Midnight invariant:** a chosen Today/Tomorrow/explicit calendar date now has one absolute market-local YYYY-MM-DD in the shared Traveler context. During foreground clock refresh, the selected day never shifts silently; the Today/Tomorrow/Pick day chip and summary adapt to that SAME saved date. It persists while the Traveler chooses a destination and returns, and clears for a genuinely new journey. Time and DST validation still use the saved market-local instant. Two-pass regression covers 23:36→00:01 midnight crossing and route-return continuity. No fare change, booking, Stripe charge, Travel creation, market status, provider subscription or live deployment.** Await exact-head CI and installed-device tests before merge/release.

**2026-10-17 ACCOUNT EMAIL WHOLE-JOURNEY CONSOLIDATION — DRAFT PR #53:** The design review found a second, outdated email-change form inside Sign-in and security that still displayed Continue and returned to idle after merely sending a link. That form is removed; Account Details and Sign-in and security now reach the same `app/account-email.tsx`, including pending/verified state, authenticated reset, real correction, and provider-specific support. The pending address is stored only in UID-scoped local storage, wiped on account switch/sign-out, with no password/token persistence; the visual design uses standard Card/PrimaryButton dimensions. Verified Firebase address changes also force a fresh identity token exactly once, so subsequent Travel and receipt requests see the new authenticated email instead of a stale ID token. Published Apple, OWASP and Firebase guidance checked. Added twice-run source contracts and kept the existing password-change route. This draft has NOT sent any email or changed a real account. The legacy separate Firestore profile `email` field is not yet certified as synchronized; Firebase auth token remains server operational authority. Require exact SHA six workflow passes, real device sign-in/link/restart/offline/wrong-password checks, and Firebase existing-email notification verification before release. [Decision ADR 0029](docs/decisions/0029-verified-email-change-user-journey.md).

**2026-10-17 VERIFIED ACCOUNT EMAIL UX — DRAFT / NOT LIVE (ADR 0029):** Founder supplied screenshot of Email address screen and repeated whole-service design rubric. Read Apple input/label HIG, OWASP sensitive identity changes, NIST account notification, Stanford user-testing guidance, council standard and real `app/account-email.tsx` / Firebase Auth. Kept shared screen, cards and primary button sizes; improved secondary-text contrast using existing ink2 rather than arbitrarily enlarging typography. Made submit action explicitly Send verification email; protect with current password reauthentication, reject same email, stop duplicate requests, and discard the entered password after sending. Distinguish link requested from verified email: pending screen displays proposed address, correction and manual status check, with on-focus/app-resume Firebase Auth `reload` and explicit revised context so the identity visibly updates only after confirmed. Existing password reset remains accessible; provider sign-in remains non-editable here with Support option, without falsely claiming an Apple/Google account change updates American Rider. No new Firestore reads, recurring checks, email vendor or invented success. All five locale copy updated and new twice-run source regression included. An account-scoped local reminder now retains the pending proposed address across app restarts (not the password or link token) and is cleared on confirmed change, correction, and account sign-out. **Unverified:** Firebase existing-address change notification, live email delivery and handoff, actual forced-restart UI behavior, device autofill/keyboard/VoiceOver, and provider-specific reauthentication; no TestFlight or deployment. [ADR 0029](docs/decisions/0029-verified-email-change-user-journey.md). Exact PR-head CI and physical tests are required before merge.

**2026-10-17 FINANCIAL TRUTH AND HISTORICAL PAYOUT CENSUS — DRAFT PR #57 (depends on unmerged #54):** Stripe account inspection: the connected sandbox (`livemode:false`) showed one $100 test PaymentIntent and one $100 test transfer without American Rider identifiers; the distinct connected live account showed no PaymentIntents or Connect transfers in its returned complete lists. No Render Stripe secret/account matching was proven and no payment was initiated. `src/backend/connect.ts` now distinguishes genuine server-confirmed unpaid Travel recovery from HTTP/transport/invalid-response uncertainty. Operator duty state tracks the latest account-bound payout check; local earnings and the Operator Revenue screen are explicitly described as device-saved estimates, never as verified Stripe transfers or bank deposits. Cached estimated earnings are now scoped by authenticated Operator UID, while the old shared-device key is left untouched but not displayed to another account. Operator payout education no longer promises immediate/automatic deposits and is localized in five languages. Added `backend/payout-recovery-census.js`: privileged, read-only bounded Firestore pages and explicit classification of completed Travel with missing, false, or true historical `payoutPending` markers. It cannot transfer money or authorize any settlement. Twice-run tests cover account/status copy and multi-page historical records. **P0 #55 and #56 remain OPEN**: historical Stripe reconciliation, durable transfer identity across retries >24 hours, lossless migration, production index READY, canonical server-backed earnings, iOS/Android and sandbox money tests are not delivered by this interim safety correction. No changes to `release/current` or Render were made. Do not merge/deploy PR #57 on the strength of unit tests alone. [Financial issue #55](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/55); [revenue issue #56](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/issues/56).

**2026-10-17 WHOLE-SERVICE AUDIT CONTINUATION, OPERATOR DUTY AND PAYOUT (DRAFT PR #54):** Secondary Conclude Operations paths (cancel an accepted operation, superseded Commence response, late renewal) now use the tracked, account-scoped server acknowledgement/retry mechanism, not a silent request; cancel also invalidates prior in-flight Commence attempts to avoid an accidental local restart of duty. Source regression tests these exact paths; two-device/offline server acceptance remains unverified. Separately, **OPEN**: payout recovery `POST /operator/settle-pending` scans all historical Operator rides every time. Do NOT restrict to only recent rides or `payoutPending:true` before an authenticated, lossless backfill and durable queue are proven; that could strand old owed fares. No live money, commission, existing debt, server availability, Firestore indexes or deployments were changed. Institution sources: Google Firestore cursor queries, AWS idempotency and Apple's truthful status feedback. [Audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md).

**2026-10-17 OPERATOR DUTY CROSS-SCREEN UTC VERIFICATION — PR #54 DRAFT:** The remaining-coverage summary in `src/state/OperatorContext.tsx` and authoritative insurance horizon use a UTC certificate end (`23:59:59.999Z`), but the `setOnline(true)` local entry precheck still parsed `23:59:59` in the phone's timezone. Corrected local precheck to exactly match the server UTC instant; twice-run source contract added. This prevents the phone giving an inconsistent account of eligibility near policy expiration, while backend verified insurer/screening checks remain authoritative. The subsequent cross-layer review also **corrected the off-duty confirmation gap in this draft**: `goOffline()` returns success only when the authenticated server replies `ok:true`, while `OperatorContext` retains a UID-scoped pending-conclusion marker across app restart, retries on resume or deliberate button press, and forbids a new Commence until the server has acknowledged the previous withdrawal. The UI explains uncertainty rather than claiming success during a network outage; the existing 44-unit primary control is reused. The server's stale-presence limit remains the independent safety backstop. **Real two-device assignment race, network restore, and native app restart tests are not complete**, so none of this is claimed production live. No server/off-duty changes deployed.

**2026-10-17 WHOLE-SERVICE AUDIT CONTINUATION — REAL OPERATOR MESSAGING AND LANGUAGE (PR #54 DRAFT):** Source cross-check found that a real active Travel was classified as a demonstration conversation in both `app/message.tsx` and `src/state/RideContext.tsx`. The former suppressed live database replies; the latter created an invented scripted Operator acknowledgement after a real outgoing message. Both now require an expressly demo-marked Operator, no persisted Travel, and no Lost Item context for scripted behavior. Real paid booked Travels use the Firestore thread and do not receive simulated Operator responses. Source-level two-pass regression added; actual signed Traveler/Operator phones, offline/reconnect and push remain unverified. Separately, the module-level translated `UNREACHABLE` Patron Support response became stale after in-app language changes; translation now occurs at the time of failed request, without added database work. Published Firestore real-time and Apple truthful-feedback guidance checked. [Audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md). All changes remain draft, no merge/deploy or real messages.

**2026-10-17 LOST ITEM OPERATOR CANDIDATE-TRAVEL CORRECTION — DRAFT PR #54:** End-to-end review found that "not sure which Travel" reports sent every nominated trip number to every Operator's detail screen, including routes that Operator never served. The backend properly rejected a later false claim but the screen still exposed invalid choices; its verified-Operator lookup also lost additional trips served by the same Operator. Current draft keeps every server-verified candidate Travel *per actual Operator* and returns only that Operator's own list. Existing UI therefore selects the only possible trip or offers a relevant choice of multiple actual trips. Deterministic mock tests assert each Operator sees only their own candidates. No new Firestore read/query or provider; retain independent server ownership verification and no invented Traveler/Operator relations. This is source/test work pending exact-head CI, production evidence and signed devices. [Cross-service audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md).

**2026-10-17 OPERATOR LOST-ITEM RECOVERY UX — PR #54 DRAFT:** Following the concurrency and durable-notification improvements, the Operator report screen was found to display an unhelpful error with no Retry when a network or authorization read failed. It now shows loading, error, explicit retry and ignores late results after leaving/reopening for a different report. The controls use existing 44-point secondary-button sizing; text is present under the Operator (not Traveler) dictionary in all five languages, with regression checking section placement. No external Operator notifications were sent during inspection. [End-to-end audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md). Require exact-head CI and signed-phone behavior before release.

**2026-10-17 CONTINUED LOST-ITEM RACE AUDIT — DRAFT PR #54:** The authenticated server could lose one of two concurrent Operator recovery responses and regress an item from `located` to `operator-notified` when a negative reply or late message-bookkeeping write landed afterward. Transactional status updates now re-read the latest report, retain multiple Operator responses, keep `located` and return states authoritative, and suppress redundant notifications after a proven find. Notification bookkeeping itself uses a server transaction, so late delivery does not overwrite a found or returned item. Adversarial simultaneous/retry/late-notice synthetic tests were added and must pass on the **exact final SHA**. **Subsequent replay correction:** initial Lost Item inbox notices now use a per-account, per-report, per-Operator stable hashed Firestore ID, with create-once semantics that avoid duplicate durable messages and pushes after client/server retries; ordinary platform notices retain independent IDs. Offline concurrent replay tests added. Device push delivery and retry-after-push-failure remain external validation gaps. [Whole-service audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md). No external notifications, money, Firebase index deployment or real accounts affected in draft.

**2026-10-17 SAFETY/SUPPORT TRAVEL-IDENTITY CORRECTION — UNMERGED PR #54:** Broad source scan of Traveler/Operator routes found the general Safety screen passed `ride.lastTrip.no` to Patron Support even when no Travel existed; this could attach an unrelated *actual prior Travel* to a new safety matter (the existing demonstration-number safeguard did not catch a previous real journey). The Travel status screen had the same direct passed-value pattern. Both now call `ride.openHelp()` so the existing shared `RideContext` selects a Travel Number only when `rideActiveRef.current` is true and otherwise leaves it blank. Twice-run cross-screen tests assert Safety, live-Travel, and shared store agree. No emergency escalation or monetary records changed. This is only a draft source correction; physically verify both between-Travel and in-Travel Support from iOS/Android before deployment. [Audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md).

**2026-10-17 WHOLE-SERVICE AUDIT CONTINUATION — OFFICIAL ACCOUNT MESSAGES AND UTC INSURANCE CUTOFF (DRAFT PR #54):** Cross-checked server durable platform messages, real Operator inbox, Traveler Account navigation, Firebase messaging, actual Lost Item notification routes, current Firestore billing guidance and the Operator dashboard versus server eligibility. Found that a Traveler's lost-item recovery notice pushed to `/operator/inbox` rather than its actual report, while per-account inbox reads loaded all historic notices before slicing, and database errors in Express 4 async handlers were not returned as controlled 503 results. Added a shared `/inbox` route reusing the existing Operator inbox component, reachable from Traveler Account; added authenticated `/account/inbox` endpoints while preserving old `/operator/inbox` URLs; corrected trusted specific-action deep links and retained old Operator default push routing for installed-app compatibility. List queries now sort and LIMIT **before** billing Firestore reads; load failures and mark-read failures return recoverable outcomes, with loading and on-demand Retry rather than false no-messages claims. Five-language Traveler/Operator copy and twice-run cross-layer source tests added. **Still needed:** generic Traveler push notice version-aware default routing and actual notification acceptance on old/new mobile builds; do not claim old builds have the new inbox. Separately, `OperatorContext` was using phone-local policy end while the server/insurance view used UTC; both app views now use the backend's exact `23:59:59.999Z` cutoff. These are UNMERGED findings, not production fixes. See [whole-service audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md). Live index readiness, rules release, Stripe sandbox, actual iOS/Android and exhaustive exact-SHA verification remain mandatory.

**2026-10-17 REPOSITORY-WIDE WHOLE-SERVICE AUDIT, FIRST VERIFIED CORRECTIONS — UNMERGED:** Founder requested repository-wide inspection of the failure patterns exposed by Schedule Travel and account email: duplicated experiences, misleading/inert actions, unbounded reads, client-forged facts, failed recovery, visual inconsistency and incomplete journeys. Enumerated Traveler/Operator app surfaces plus shared clients and backend; reviewed source evidence against Council standards, UI baselines, published Stripe documentation, Google Firestore pagination, and Nielsen Norman Group error guidance. New draft branch corrects five directly verified defects: (1) Wallet hid Remove for a default saved card; an owned default card now has explicit confirmation and Stripe default-pointer cleanup before detach, with clear five-language consequences and no live Stripe actions; (2) Firestore lost-item creation accepted forged initial status/return; client creation now restricted to reported/null return with bounded fields, adversarial emulator cases added; critically, backend notification, private report reading and Operator response now also independently verify the nominated Operator against real server Travel ownership (one bounded candidate query), overwriting untrusted phone-nominated IDs and rejecting another Traveler's Travel; (3) Lost-item history read every record then returned false empty on failure; bounded 20-record ordered pages, explicit error and a declared new composite index now replace that scan. The Patron Support caller now shows a recoverable failure rather than an empty list or unhandled rejection, retains previously shown reports, and loads the next page only when requested. (4) Account Mobile silently displayed an empty field after a failed Firestore read and allowed editing before determining the original value; now it shows loading/unavailable with Retry and blocks overwriting unknown account data, retaining shared control sizing in all five languages. (5) Lost Item messages could quietly substitute the seeded/last Travel Number if a real report lacked a linked trip; the filed report now alone determines the linked thread, and unlinked reports are sent to Support with no invented Travel. The message route also refuses to listen/send without a real trip. **Index is NOT certified READY in production.** Existing separate drafts #52 (scheduled reservation cancel/write authority and bounded lists) and #53 (verified email changes) must be reconciled, not overwritten. The [cross-service audit](docs/audits/WHOLE-SERVICE-INVARIANTS-2026-10-17.md) states the inspected surfaces and unreviewed backend/device/provider work; do not claim the entire repository exhaustively verified from a first-pass scan. Mobile contact verification, Operator polling and runtime provider gates remain investigations, not assumptions. No merge, deploy, live payment, Firebase index or device release authorized by these draft edits.

**2026-10-16 GUIDED OPERATOR AND MULTI-QUOTE INSURANCE UX — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** PR building on the open-first correction implements practical improvements from the end-to-end usability audit. The existing insurance screen now places state requirements and shareable broker wording in plain view, exposes genuine external multi-carrier (Insureon) and three-independent-agent (Limousine Insurance HQ) quote-request paths, then offers optional specialist carriers, with no preferential single broker, no claimed instant prices and clear limits for older vehicles. The separate Documents screen now routes insurance to that one guidance/review route. The qualification checklist now shows a separate editable **Vehicle** entry instead of the obsolete self-verifying Basics badge; entering name/car/plate never grants approval. The vehicle description requests its model year in five languages for accurate carrier eligibility questions. The background screen already exposes new and existing check options and broken provider-link recovery from the preceding market-correction branch. Added twice-run safeguards and [ADR 0028](docs/decisions/0028-first-class-operator-insurance-and-onboarding.md). **A cancelled or pending-cancelled approved insurance policy may now be replaced even before its printed expiry, avoiding a trapped Operator. No insurer quotes, new provider contract, underwriting/vehicle-age exception, approval, paid bookings, price changes or actual TestFlight deployment are claimed.** Await current-head checks and actual devices/provider evidence.

**2026-10-16 FIRST-CLASS OPERATOR ONBOARDING UX — PR CANDIDATE ON OPEN-FIRST BRANCH:** Actual mobile insurance screen now displays concise coverage guidance and Florida statutory minimums above the action; offers one-tap sharing of the existing authoritative jurisdiction-specific broker script; surfaces external multi-quote Insureon, three-broker Limousine Insurance HQ and specialist Progressive prominently, with owned-broker choice and secondary providers on demand. Removes the prominent single stale-contact Garzor panel without claiming an exclusive provider or guaranteeing cheapest premiums. Displays older-vehicle guidance as conditional insurer underwriting, not guaranteed eligibility; preserves primary source status verification, insurer emails, cancellation notices, original document review, legal duties and state policy rules. Removes one-tap `Basics` from the apparent verified compliance checklist (not a legal identity check), adds immediate Stripe payout navigation and market retry, distinguishes network errors from genuine closed markets and failed review status, restores broken CRA website/retry, and adds truthful PDF/photo 5 MB guidance. Fixes a discovered Traveler lost-item photo regression from the shared R2 upload route by gating only Operator document uploads by active market while retaining fresh authentication, rate limits and private ownership for both purposes. All five locale files updated; twice-run source regressions added. This is a **draft nested atop open-first PR #48**, not merged or deployed. No source-verified CRA/insurer handoff, live quote API, subscribed insurance provider, external procurement, TestFlight or real handset evidence is invented. Do not merge without exact-head CI and device acceptance. [UX audit](docs/audits/OPERATOR-ONBOARDING-END-TO-END-UX-2026-10-16.md).

**2026-10-16 FOUNDER SETTLED POLICY — SERVICE OPEN FIRST, THEN OPERATOR REGISTRATION (ADR 0027):** Founder corrected an unauthorized proposal to prepare applications in unopened counties. Closed, unmerged PR #47; do not merge or resurrect it. Operator registration, screening, document and PDF upload/review, insurance source processing, Stripe payout setup, qualification submission, going on duty and paid Travel start only when the relevant geographically configured county is ACTIVE/open, with independent personal qualifications still strictly enforced. Unopened counties, unsupported states such as Colorado and unknown geography receive a clear Coming soon experience, with a low-cost interest list only when the user explicitly opts in. Merely selecting a county never enrolls somebody in a waitlist. The source had permitted a background-screening exception in waitlisted Florida and automatically recorded interest at county selection; corrective draft closes the exception and removes silent waitlist enrollment, preserves cheap opt-in interest. The existing Traveler home already shows an unavailable-market explanation and a separate account-bound rate-limited interest action. The waitlist endpoint stores a record, but an actual market-launch email/notification delivery process has NOT been proven: do not promise verified future delivery. No duplicate retired `market_admission` administrative gate. No fees, market statuses, background report purchases, external approvals or TestFlight changed. Candidate code requires six exact-head checks and signed iPhone/Android verification before release. [ADR 0027](docs/decisions/0027-open-first-operator-onboarding-and-waitlist.md).

**2026-10-16 OPERATOR ONBOARDING FIRST-CLASS UX AUDIT (ADVISORY ONLY):** Traced current commercial Operator economics, qualification, profile/vehicle, private license/registration/PDF evidence, insurance, screening, statutory disclosure, payouts, review, commissioning, duty and separate waitlist. Audit recommends a single accurate application checklist, unambiguous statuses and error recovery, old-vehicle treatment without a fictitious statutory age ban, shareable insurance requirements, neutral multi-broker comparison paths, source-verified contact information and explicit opt-in. Insureon and Limousine Insurance HQ provide external *requests for quotes*, not an American Rider live quote comparison or certified cheaper premium. Current referred broker Garzor published phone/hours differ from app; verify and correct under a separate carefully tested UI PR. Real CRA report handoff, independent insurer confirmation, actual quote comparability, interest notification fulfillment, physical-device reliability remain unverified; no fake claims. [Source audit](docs/audits/OPERATOR-ONBOARDING-END-TO-END-UX-2026-10-16.md). This is a list of proposals, not a redesign or provider endorsement.

**2026-10-16 ONE-FOUNDER OPERATOR SCREENING PHONE RECOVERY — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** The pending qualification screen previously queried backend qualification including external status every 30 seconds, regardless of being visible, adding avoidable reads. New foreground/focus/phone-return refresh and two-minute interval reduce idle load by approximately 4x during a continuously open screen; it does not replace external evidence or certify Firebase throughput. The insurance screen also failed to refresh after a broker response until the Operator manually refreshed it; a focus/phone-resume refresh is included. The screening screen fetched status only once, so returning to the same phone from an external CRA site did not refresh status unless the Operator navigated away. It also called a screening "verified" until its exact expiry, but the server blocks duty 24 hours in advance. Corrected the screen to refresh on focus and phone resume, refresh pending states at most once every two minutes while foregrounded, and align the visible renewal threshold with the server's 24-hour advance lockout. Adds statutory screening regression for the threshold and source-level UI guards. **More important, separately:** the provider-neutral new/existing-report path currently creates a durable Operations case; live secure CRA report-to-app intake is *not* wired to a partner. That means the founder still has a manual exception to process for new screening reports until an actual authenticated CRA handoff is commissioned. Do not advertise this as a fully automated screening provider. Existing independent insurance source verification, monthly refresh reminders, and Operator duty lockouts are implemented but cannot be taken as evidence of live broker response without verified provider records. No fake approvals, provider contract, pricing, billing tier, live money, or market-state changes.\n\n
**2026-10-16 PRELAUNCH RELEASE EVIDENCE CORRECTION — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Source check identified a legacy manual commercial-evidence validator that incorrectly demanded active Smart Travel and real-money reconciliation before an ordinary Travel launch. Founder explicitly chose grey/disabled new Smart bookings and prelaunch Stripe sandbox proof, not paid transit service or public passenger charges. Candidate removes mandatory live-transit and Smart-live evidence, verifies the server/client remain explicitly paused, and substitutes specific independently attested **signed-app Stripe sandbox** quote-to-booking-to-payment-to-dispatch/retry/refund evidence for the old live-money field. It also removes experimental pricing holdout as a launch requirement while retaining ratified local pricing review, actual Stripe account readiness, insurer/screening source verification, device acceptance, load measures, and restricted-facility legality. A sandbox run is NOT claimed completed by this change. No provider settings, market configuration, fares, permission grants or live payments changed. [ADR 0025](docs/decisions/0025-prelaunch-release-evidence-scope.md).\n\n

**2026-10-16 VERIFIED EAS SIMULATOR WORKER FIX — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Founder requested completion of remaining mobile, sandbox-payment, and Firebase-capacity evidence. The prior manual-only native test workflow lacked worker selection; official Expo EAS documents `macos-medium` for iOS Simulator and `linux-medium-nested-virtualization` for Android Emulator. Candidate assigns these explicit workers with fail-fast test assertions, without triggering billable EAS runs. GitHub automation already passed full synthetic Traveler/Operator Travel, signed offline simulated Stripe events, double whole-platform regression, and scale economics; those **do not prove installed-app Stripe sandbox, physical handset, or live server throughput**. Render server metrics show one Starter instance under low prelaunch load; actual Firebase billing/usage inaccessible from authenticated current tools. Remaining evidence is narrow: a signed build in an isolated test environment completing PaymentSheet-to-dispatch-to-Operator-to-receipt/refund; EAS iOS/Android emulator test runs and real iPhone/Android permission/background behavior; Firebase plan and measured isolated load. Original insurance/CRA source verification and relevant external permits remain independent sign-offs, not made true by synthetic fixtures. No pricing, service geography, activation bureaucracy, real-money configuration, or vendor subscription changes.\n\n
**2026-10-16 PRELAUNCH PAYMENTS/CAPACITY/NATIVE EXPERIENCE — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** Existing synthetic Stripe tests already verify signatures, durable receipt/retry, refunds, disputes, Connect account restriction and failure recovery **offline**, twice; these do not send Stripe network calls or simulate the full signed mobile wallet-to-bank path. The connected Stripe sandbox contains one successful PaymentIntent created by Testing Blueprints; it is not American Rider end-to-end evidence. The deployed South Florida backend remains one Render Starter instance; Firebase billing plan cannot be confirmed through current integrations. Live source renews Operator duty at 90,000 ms in foreground; 100 eight-hour shifts would make ~32,000 check-in attempts, each normally producing at least one fleet write, compared with Firestore free 20,000 writes/day; background location and bookings can add more. No supported simultaneous-user count without isolated measured load; no billing changes or live synthetic traffic allowed. Adds a twice-run capacity/payment evidence regression and a **manual-only** Expo EAS Maestro smoke workflow for iOS simulator and Android emulator (welcome, fictional email-to-sign-in and back), not a physical-device sign-off and not an automated chargeable build. The definitive device path must also include signed-device payment sheet, background duty/GPS, insurance PDF picker, reviewer status, recovery and support. Council source lens: Google Firestore billing/emulators, AWS retries, NASA intended-context V&V, Expo EAS Maestro, Apple UI tests, Render metrics/scale. No prices, market state, operator eligibility or live financial settings changed.\n\n
**2026-10-15 OPERATOR PDF MIME FALLBACK — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** A double-escaped PDF filename expression in `src/backend/documentUpload.ts` did not recognize an ordinary `.pdf` filename when a native file picker omitted MIME type. This change fixes the expression and adds PDF filename, query suffix, and non-PDF regressions. Existing 5 MiB, private storage, independent review and actual insurer verification safeguards remain. Require CI, merge, deployed source check and signed-device Files picker validation. No payment or policy changes.


**2026-10-15 ONBOARDING INSURANCE SOURCE BYPASS AND PHONE DEAD END — RELEASE CANDIDATE:** Source trace of a normal Operator showed every OCR insurance image/PDF stays `review` until named Operations approves it via `documents.insurance.decision.verdict=accept`. The server erroneously checked only the earlier raw `verdict=accept` when requiring independent current insurer status, creating a possible qualification bypass; its authorization/attestation routes also refused the same human-approved document, and the phone hid independent broker-verification steps when the local badge remained `todo`. Candidate fixes all four, resets old continuing insurance status on **every** new insurance document upload (even one held for review), exposes only server-reviewed document approval and expiry, and tests incomplete, insurer-confirmed and subsequently cancelled coverage. Independent original insurer confirmation remains required, no synthetic paper is ever marked genuine, and no change to founder's Operator-funded policy, new vendor, tariff, market admission or money. Require six exact-head CI workflows, merge and Render commit match. [ADR 0024](docs/decisions/0024-human-insurance-source-gate.md).

**2026-10-15 OPERATOR RESTART REVIEW RECOVERY — FIX CANDIDATE:** After document uploads, the phone's transient held-for-review flags disappear after a restart; the Operator's qualification Submit button was then disabled even though original evidence remained in private R2 and on the backend. This change leaves the Submit button available inside an authorized operating area and makes the authoritative `/operator/qualification/submit` decide what is missing, held or qualified, without any self-approval. Adds restart regression and preserves the five-language and prelaunch area checks. Exact-head six workflow tests, merge, Render verification and new signed phone validation remain necessary. [ADR 0023](docs/decisions/0023-operator-qualification-restart.md). No payment, insurance, screening or area gate changes.


**2026-10-15 OPERATOR NATIVE FILES/PDF SUPPORT — RELEASE CANDIDATE:** The iPhone/Android “Choose a file” action only opened Photos, and the private R2 service refused PDF files. Candidate adds the Expo SDK 57 native Files picker with PDF/image selection, strict 5 MiB cap, account-owned R2 upload, and `application/pdf` *only* for Operator documents. PDF evidence stays `review` until original is examined by named Operations; a document is never self-verified and untrusted screenshots/reports do not approve screening or live insurance. New synthetic tests cover permitted storage type, wrong-purpose refusal, true Files picker and PDF-to-human-review. Requires all six exact-head CI workflows, merge, Render exact SHA, and a new signed iOS/Android binary and physical-file-upload test; JavaScript bundle export alone is not native validation. Existing screening vendor handoff and insurance-source proof remain unverified, as does the production Firebase billing plan. Capacity: at one Operator update each 90 seconds, 100 eight-hour shifts could create ~32,000 document writes a day, above the Standard free daily 20k writes; if production is Spark, authorizing metered Blaze with an explicit spending alert is the smallest capacity prerequisite, but no charge/plan change is authorized or made in this candidate. Do not upgrade Render without load evidence. [ADR 0022](docs/decisions/0022-operator-native-pdf-documents.md). No fare, geography, payout, screening policy or separate county gate change.

**2026-10-15 ORIGINAL OPERATOR EVIDENCE REVIEW — FIX CANDIDATE:** An Operator uploads a licence, registration or insurance photo to PRIVATE R2, and `backend/server.js` stores only an `objectKey`. All OCR readings intentionally remain on human review until independently verified; however the Operations exception page linked obsolete `d.imageUrl`, so reviewers could not open the uploaded originals. This candidate adds a named/MFA Operations-only, owner-bound, 5 MiB limited document viewer with server-only R2 access (no signed link exposure) and synthetic regressions. No paper is automatically accepted; no insurance, screening, Stripe or Fare gates change. **Separate gaps:** mobile “Choose a file” is an image picker and does not yet support PDF; actual source-verified insurance and CRA handoffs, signed device upload, production Firebase budget and load are not independently verified. Capacity next: if Firestore billing is Spark, enabling Blaze usage billing with budget alerts is the first commercial scaling step, not an automatic Render tier upgrade; 100 Operators for eight hours renewing every 90 seconds could consume ~32,000 fleet writes before bookings (assumption, not actual bill). Confirm live plan/usage and run isolated representative load tests before changing the 0.5 CPU/512 MB Render instance. [ADR 0021](docs/decisions/0021-private-operator-document-review.md). This paragraph is a candidate until exact-head CI, merge and deployment are verified.\n\n**2026-10-15 LOST-ITEM OPERATOR SEARCH — AUDIT/FIX CANDIDATE:** Source audit found `/travel/return-operator` read the entire Operator collection for each lost-item return attempt. A small candidate replaces that with one direct lookup for the original Operator, then the existing bounded geographic search for an eligible alternate; relationship checks, insurance/screening gates and authoritative alternate-return quote are unchanged. Scale regression now counts the initial 40 and fallback 120 Firestore documents from each saturated geohash cell: up to **2,880 billed reads/search** (not the former 2,160 returned docs model) and **5,760 across prepare + dispatch** in the fully saturated extreme. Theoretical Standard-free allocation is then only **8 Travels/day** at zero idle usage, NOT certified throughput or registered-user limit. Exact-head six-workflow CI, PR merge, backend deployment remain required. Family age-out source fix PR #16 remains on hold pending production index commissioning. See ADR [0020](docs/decisions/0020-lost-item-geo-capacity-budget.md).


**2026-10-15 FOUNDER PRELAUNCH SMART TRAVEL PAUSE — REVIEW CANDIDATE:** American Rider has **not commercially launched**. Founder directs Smart Travel displayed grey/“currently unavailable,” without new Smart booking or paid transit infrastructure. PR candidate disables new Smart quote on client and server (503 `smart_travel_paused`, safe 404 for older clients), disables selectable card, blocks first Smart car at action boundary and direct navigation; preserves existing Smart software, second-leg defensive recovery and ordinary Travel. Source regression protects no extra OTP/Firestore request or fare change. This is not evidence of any current paying passengers. Existing $7 Render is one 0.5 CPU/512MB instance without autoscaling; free Standard Firestore Spark daily document quota is 50k reads/20k writes, but billing state and daily usage cannot be verified from available source access. The prior no-user Firestore quota incident prevents claiming a specific capacity. Arithmetic model `backend/paid-scale-economics.test.js` assumes 100 reads/40 writes per Travel; theoretical **500 Travels/day at ZERO background use** is not a tested capacity. No reliable concurrent-user count without a measured baseline and isolated representative stress test. ADR `docs/decisions/0019-smart-travel-prelaunch-pause-and-capacity.md`. No added charges or market/fare/Operator permission changes.\n\n

**2026-10-15 SMART BUS BOARDING/SEASONAL SERVICE PROFILES — MERGED, BACKEND AND WEB DEPLOYED; REAL FEED/ROUTER NOT LIVE:** Source audit found that the September 10 South Florida bus allow-list was produced from a weekday timetable at each route's *busiest* stop; it cannot certify weekday vs Saturday/Sunday service or the wait at the actual boarding stop, and it is not a live feed. The candidate preserves every current rail route and accepted legacy route file, adds transit-region-local weekday/Saturday/Sunday profiles (15-minute weekday headway, up to 30 minutes Saturday/Sunday), and rejects any proposed bus itinerary if the scheduled wait at the precise boarding stop exceeds 15 minutes on a weekday from 07:00–19:00 or 30 minutes at other times. A new offline, three-bus-agency GTFS compiler fails closed if an agency feed or service-day profile cannot be validated, does not publish a partial profile, and runs without paying a transit provider. Pure safety tests include local-time/DST and transfer cases; an end-to-end synthetic test builds three independent bus agency GTFS feeds for three days while train feeds are deliberately absent, twice, verifies schedule selection and no overwrite on failure. **Verified release:** [Commercial PR #20](https://github.com/chadkhalil21-svg/American-Rider-Commercial/pull/20) passed all seven exact-head CI checks (including two exhaustive runs and stress checks), merged into `release/current` at `5c354d311d56fc962f5dfa1a141ecf030da956ee`, and Render backend, website and preview were independently confirmed LIVE on that identical commit (backend deploy `dep-db8nkeeq1p3s738hd1fg`). No additional paid service, fares, Operator terms, county approval or payment changed. **Important limits:** The repository still contains the older single-day list until authentic refreshed GTFS feeds are obtained and this compiler is used, OTP graph is rebuilt from exactly those sources, and the results are deployed/tested. The candidate's 30-minute stop-wait policy alone does NOT yet add routes that only operate on weekends. Live OTP, schedule freshness (historical Tri-Rail sample expires 20 October 2026), real-time disruption feeds, actual same-stop access, signed-device travel, fares and physical transfer all remain unverified. No fare/payout/provider/market change, new subscription or live payment. Source guidance: GTFS official feed specification/best practices, OpenTripPlanner realtime updaters, Google SRE operations, Amazon retry consistency and NASA intended-environment V&V. Source/code verification, automated checks, actual provider data and physical devices remain distinct types of evidence.
**2026-10-15 REAL OPERATOR MAP POSITION — CANDIDATE, NOT DEPLOYED:** Independent live screen audit found the iPhone map interpolated a visually moving car from server journey status over a road route, despite the Operator already reporting GPS to the authoritative ride document every ~15 seconds. A real car could appear at a position not independently reported by its device. ADR 0018 proposes to reuse the EXISTING Firestore Travel listener to expose only fresh, validated Operator GPS (<60 seconds), refuse demo/released/completed/stale coordinates, hide the real-car marker when position is unknown, and never open the trip map at fixed Miami when route endpoints are absent. Demonstration-only operators retain their explicitly simulated maps. No new Firestore listener/read, route vendor, expense, fare, or user tracking. Added synthetic GPS/state/race regression. The Android/web six-second loop previously displayed a fabricated moving car even for a real Travel; candidate instead uses the existing MapLibre route view for real Android journeys with NO simulated vehicle marker, and omits that animation from real web journeys. **Actual signed-device/background GPS, Android live vehicle marker implementation, full Release Gate and Render deployment remain independent requirements; no claim of commercial commissioning.** Council source rubric: Google Firestore reads, Amazon retries, NASA real-operating validation, Apple truthful location cues.

**2026-10-15 NATIONALLY NEUTRAL PICKUP-PIN ORIGIN — CANDIDATE, TEST REQUIRED:** Code inspection found that iPhone's manual pickup-pin map defaulted to fixed downtown Miami when GPS was denied or unavailable, contradicting the nationally reusable map and risking a wrong pickup. Proposed ADR 0017 removes the Miami origin, opens at a previously selected valid pin or explicitly resolved departure, otherwise requests new location and shows a translated address-search alternative if unavailable. It refuses non-finite coordinates; reverse-geocodes the untouched initial map center; blocks confirmation until the current pin has a label; and guards against late geocode replies overwriting a newly moved location. New pure TypeScript helper and backend synthetic test validate selected-pickup priority, non-Miami coordinates, invalid GPS, empty GPS, and UI safeguards. **Not yet merged/deployed or physical-iPhone certified.** Android pin drop and browser live street map are separate existing gaps. No added map provider, payment, fare, region, or third-party cost. Source: Apple Core Location permission guidance; Google no-manual-toil; Amazon retry consistency; NASA intended-environment validation.

**2026-10-15 SMART SECOND-CAR PHONE-RESTART RECOVERY — UNDER TEST:** After the single-use backend safety fix PR #15, source review found the mobile app still generated a fresh random booking key for each second-car confirmation; a crash/restart could create a different key and be rejected by the one-use server lock, stranding the Traveler. This candidate derives the second-car booking identity deterministically from the *real server-issued* first-leg Travel Number, scoped by the authenticated account on the server. The app refuses to begin a second car with an invalid/placeholder first-leg number and uses the same stable booking key on dispatch retries; ordinary Travel retains independent random booking keys. Backend synthetic regression compiles and executes the real helper, tests phone restarts, bad ids, per-user identity and confirms the call-site gate. No new server reads, pricing change or third-party calls. **Still unresolved:** if the same prepared second-car Travel is explicitly cancelled or permanently fails payment, the same Travel needs an authorized recovery or customer-support path; never remove the first-leg one-use claim or create another charged Travel without a separately audited recovery process. This does not certify an actual signed-phone restart, OTP route graph, real-time GTFS service, transport transfer or Stripe production settlement. CI, merge and Render deploy must be checked separately.


**2026-10-15 SMART TRAVEL ONE-USE AUTHORITY — SYNTHETIC FIX UNDER PR REVIEW:** Source audit found two gaps in Smart continuation: (1) the first-leg query checked an obsolete top-level `journeyNo` even though actual second-leg records store `journey.journeyNo`, allowing a second leg to be chained again; (2) simultaneous separate booking keys could each claim the same completed first car. Candidate repairs both without changing existing settled fares or normal Travel: it accepts only a recent (within 48 hours), paid, completed and unrefunded original first car; obtains its real Firestore id; and atomically records the single second-car booking on that original document in the same transaction that creates the second Travel. The same booking-key retry remains idempotent; different keys cannot reserve a duplicate second car. Synthetic regression models 40 simultaneous second-car attempts, stale/refunded/foreign/chained legs and server-inherited Teen party. A second booking after a permanently failed initial second-car payment needs a deliberate safe recovery workflow; do not silently release the single-use claim or create another charge. This is source/Council-guided review using Google Firestore limited reads, Amazon idempotent workflows, OWASP object authorization, NASA intended-environment tests. CI/merge/deployment and actual signed-device transit handoff remain separate evidence. No OTP graph, real-time transit provider, paid hosting, new legal record or live money commissioned by this change.


**2026-10-15 ANDROID OPERATOR BACKGROUND LOCATION PRIVACY — CANDIDATE:** Google Play requires a prominent explanation of background location and a clear choice before requesting runtime permission. The former Operator commute-to-duty path did not present this. Candidate adds Android-only, five-language text and Not now/Continue prior to foreground and background location requests. Declining keeps Operator off duty and requests no new position; already-granted background permissions avoid repeated dialogs. No Traveler fare, county admission, Stripe, insurance or screening change. This is source review plus proposed tests, NOT Android physical-device or Google Play approval. ADR 0016, backend/android-location-consent.test.js; full six campaigns and device validation pending.


**2026-10-15 CONTROLLED ANDROID RELEASE PREPARATION — UNDER REVIEW:** Source/Expo/GitHub documentation review found Android EAS production configuration already exists but a controlled exact-commit Android Play bundle build workflow is missing. PR candidate adds a manually triggered, exact-release-verified Android app-bundle build plus a test; it does not publish, execute a paid cloud build automatically or certify a device. The existing iOS manual TestFlight workflow exists only on `release/current`, while the repository's default branch remains the stale `approved/account-security-2026-09-27`; GitHub requires manual workflows to exist on the default branch. Aligning the default branch to `release/current` is a one-time repository setting action not performed here. Play Console approval, background-location disclosure and real devices remain unverified. See `docs/decisions/0015-controlled-android-store-build.md` and Commercial launch Issue #8. No fare/market/insurance/security policy change.


**2026-10-14 RELEASE EVIDENCE VENDOR-NEUTRALITY — PR UNDER TEST:** Audited production release evidence against D03: the release validator, template and regression contract mistakenly required a `checkr` evidence key despite provider-neutral, Operator-funded screening. This candidate consistently renames that requirement `screeningProvider`, preserves independent authentic screening-provider proof and the remaining release gates, and adds regression checks that reject a vendor-named gate. Existing manifests using `checkr` must migrate their evidence metadata before passing this updated validator; no screening report is fabricated, no release is certified, and no market, fare, Operator or payment authorization changes. Source-led audit follows Google SRE eliminating manual toil, AWS controlled/idempotent operations and NASA V&V. CI, merge and Render deployment must be independently checked.

**2026-10-14 OPERATOR INSURANCE EXPERIENCE / COUNCIL SOURCE REVIEW — CANDIDATE:** The founder correctly questioned whether adding permanent visible nonpayment/cancellation controls meant the product was well designed. We checked `app/operator/insurance.tsx` and `src/components/operator.tsx`: the Operator sees those choices only after opening the persistent **Insurance** menu route; there is **no automatic daily, shift-start, on-duty, or every-Travel question**, no app-known signal for whether a premium was unpaid, and no verified iOS TestFlight binary of PR #194's native UI. The server auto-rejects known invalid insurance at qualification/dispatch/acceptance, but monthly authenticated provider requests do not imply an always-live third-party cancellation feed. Reviewed [Apple HIG Alerts](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/alerts), [Apple HIG Notifications](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/notifications), [Nielsen Norman 10 heuristics](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/ten-usability-heuristics/), [NN/g Progressive Disclosure](https://www.nngroup.com/videos/progressive-disclosure/), [Google SRE Eliminating Toil](https://sre.google/workbook/eliminating-toil/), [Microsoft Reliability Monitoring](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/well-architected/reliability/monitoring), [Stanford d.school](https://dschool.stanford.edu/stories/integrative-design-a-practice-to-tackle-complex-challenges), and [UK service design](https://www.gov.uk/service-manual/service-standard/point-4-make-the-service-simple-to-use). Derived design, not an endorsement: do **not** demand daily premium attestations, scare Operators on every shift or pretend to have access to insurer payment records. Keep exceptional self-report permanently discoverable, not prominent, with one concise expandable **Report a change to my insurance** link, two clear choices only after expansion, and confirmation before a real safety stop. If a carrier reports cancellation, proactively show existing actionable hold state; routine monitoring remains invisible when healthy. Change status copy to avoid falsely attributing an Operator's self-report to an insurer. Keep actual reported hold/verification logic unchanged. Automated synthetic tests run twice, but **real iPhone accessibility and Operator usability tests remain unverified**. Pricing unchanged, no exclusive insurer, no paperwork invented, no market activation claimed. Source audit: `docs/audits/INSURANCE-REPORTING-UX-COUNCIL-2026-10-14.md`.

**2026-10-14 NONPAYMENT/UNEXPECTED CANCELLATION POLICY & INDEPENDENT STATUS MONITORING — PR UNDER REVIEW:** **Founder decision remains final**: Operators pay for and maintain verified commercial livery/TNC-use cover; American Rider stops new duty, offers and acceptance on known disqualification. **Early cancellation is different from the printed expiration date**. Reviewed Florida 2026 §627.728/§627.7281: certain policies require 10 days' pre-notice for nonpayment to the insured/agent, **not necessarily to American Rider**. There is no universally accessible free real-time carrier-payment or cancellation API. EXISTING mechanisms already include carrier/broker status choice `pending_cancellation`, `cancelled`, immediate fleet suspension, current insurer verification, expiry buffer and monthly status refresh. Newly added: one clearly labeled self-report control on Operator Insurance in all five supported languages; an authenticated Operator who receives a nonpayment/cancellation notice may instantly block THEIR OWN new Travels via server transaction, with audit, no premium/payment data collected, no paid vendor integration. Provider recovery requires a new verified source response issued AFTER that notice; stale active links cannot defeat the hold. Synthetic double-pass tests cover early nonpayment, cancelled, incorrect old insurer response, re-verification, and fail-closed behavior; full CI and live deploy MUST be checked before declaring complete. Existing incident support for ongoing Travels is unchanged. This reduces exposure but **cannot guarantee knowledge of an unreported cancellation**, and neither an internal Founder approval nor green synthetic tests prove missing coverage or screening vendor delivery; do not mark `company_insurance_bound` or `SCREENING_EVIDENCE_WORKFLOW_VERIFIED` as sourced. South Florida remains subject to the authenticated production market-admission state, with founder's standing one-command opening instruction; do not invent government sign-offs, insurance certificates, transaction success, or active counties. Research references and exact audit: `docs/audits/NONPAYMENT-CANCELLATION-2026-10-14.md`. Prior $1+$0.95/mile+$0.32/min and 99% Operator Fare decisions unchanged.

**2026-10-14 FOUNDER APPROVAL + ADVANCE INSURANCE EXPIRY — PR UNDER REVIEW, NOT DEPLOYED:** Founder explicitly APPROVES the four project/business areas previously described (insurance/operating authority, Operator screening/qualification, Travel/payments, safety/release); this is a standing founder management sign-off for completed design/work and must not trigger repeated approval requests. **Do not confuse this executive sign-off with independently verified market authorization, insurer contracts, authenticated screening handoffs, physical-device results, or completion of missing production records.** Founder reaffirmed the policy that Operators individually pay for independently verified, qualifying commercial livery/TNC-use insurance; no company coverage purchase or exclusive screening-provider enrollment is authorized. When cover is known to lapse, Operators must never receive or accept new Travel. Current code checks actual expiry when it happens but did not consistently check how soon it would happen relative to a new Travel. This PR proposes a single conservative **24-hour pre-expiry lockout** for new duty, dispatch and acceptance, plus an additional **estimated route minutes + 1-hour** buffer for long Travels. The 24h/hour values are American Rider operational safeguards, **not statutory timings**. Persist already server-authoritative routed minutes in the booking for the acceptance guard; reject date-only malformed/missing or short-lived fleet evidence at real dispatch; allow an independently verified renewal to resume naturally. An unexpected insurer cancellation can never be promised detected beforehand without actual insurer notice, and an already accepted Travel remains a Travel under §627.748(1)(b). **No policy is newly purchased; do not mark `company_insurance_bound` or `SCREENING_EVIDENCE_WORKFLOW_VERIFIED` as proven without actual source.** The protected founder 'open South Florida' standing command remains authorized and self-retrying; no county is declared open until the protected database responds successfully. Published design sources and exact tests: [October 14 advance-expiry audit](docs/audits/INSURANCE-ADVANCE-EXPIRY-2026-10-14.md). This supersedes any statement that a mere expiry-at-now check necessarily prevents a mid-Travel known expiry, but does not assert prevention of unannounced retroactive lapses. Price unchanged.

**2026-10-13 ACTUAL FARE PROPOSAL—NON-LIVE, FOUNDERS' PRICE DECISION REQUIRED:** A simple South Florida Standard candidate is now quantified and checked, based on public no-surge price-model observations, official Miami-Dade taxi rate as DISTINCT context, IRS $0.76/mile cost sensitivity, national BLS May-2025 taxi-driver wage comparator, and UC Berkeley published unpaid-time study. Proposed coefficients: $1.00 base + $0.95/routed mile + $0.32/routed minute plus existing dynamically sufficient Platform Fee, minimum Fare $3 and government/toll pass-throughs; 99% of Fare goes to Operator. Domestic no-pass-through route examples: 3mi/10min $9.55; 5mi/15min $13.05; 8mi/21min $17.82; 10mi/25min **$21.00** (vs old $15.75); these are NOT LIVE prices and not independently verified current competitor quotes. The 10mi/25min proposal matches a public June-2026 Miami *model* range ~$20.15–$21.20, not a real-time quote, and is a plausible research candidate, not guaranteed 10% savings. Driver net after a hypothetical vehicle allowance of 76 cents including 20% empty miles equals ~$22.08 per passenger-occupied hour but only ~$14.35 per full on-duty hour if 65% utilized; real insurance, empty travel, idle-time outcomes remain unverified. Improving demand density/dispatch is essential—price alone cannot buy idle-time wage guarantees without uncompetitive fares. Detailed [proposed schedule, sources and hold points](docs/audits/SOUTH-FLORIDA-CONCRETE-PRICE-CANDIDATE-2026-10-13.md), simulated in `backend/florida-price-candidate.test.js`. **Do NOT merge actual fare change** from 100/85/15 to 100/95/32 until live matched evidence/Operator unit costs, physical payment and user agreement readiness, local legal gates and founder approval. No states beyond Florida inherit coefficients. This proposal does not change active money.

**2026-10-13 UNIVERSITY / PLATFORM PRICE AUDIT + STALE-EVIDENCE CLOSURE — RESEARCH-GATED CHANGE:** Stanford GSB, Columbia/Harvard/CMU, MIT research favors local geography, Operator supply/utilization, stable price learning using genuine bookings, not blindly undercutting rivals. Google data-quality guidance and Firestore cost guidance exposed a production evidence-lineage defect: a 90-day pool with only the most recent observation checked could let 19 obsolete prices plus one fresh price appear sufficiently current and distort the condition median. Pending safe PR restricts every eligible source observation to 30 days or the stricter market plan, records oldest/newest timestamps, rejects unproven old promoted snapshots, and cuts obsolete 90-day scans to 30 days. No live Travel coefficients, Operator share, customer total, price-learning automation, supplier contracts, money, insurance, admissions or state rollouts change. Historical snapshots and model estimates do not become commercial prices. Confirm six exact-head campaigns and production Render SHA after merge, with separate device/payment/field evidence outstanding.
